2. **Immediate neighboring nodes of P**:
   - Neighbors: I, Q

- R is connected to F. The neighbors of F are B, E, S.
- P is connected to I. The neighbors of I are H, G, Q.

Now, we need to find the shortest path between these nodes:

- By examining the connections:
  - If we follow from R to F, we need to check the connections from F to the nodes that connect to P.
  - We see that the neighbors of F (B, E, S) do not directly connect to P or its neighbors. So, we check the next connections:
    - B is connected to E, J, N, C, T
    - G, neighbor of I, connects to H, B, C, F

These connections do not directly connect R and P.

To find a path:
- R -> F -> B -> C -> G -> I -> P

Length: 5

Therefore, the shortest path between R and P is:

Length: 5, Path: [R, F, B, C, G, I, P]

1. **Identify the two black nodes:** \( E \) and \( R \).
2. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of \( E \) and \( R \):**
   - **Neighbors of \( E \):** A, B, and J
   - **Neighbors of \( R \):** P, Q, O, and G
3. **Identify if any of the neighbors overlap:**
   - No immediate overlap is found.
4. **Trace the paths through intermediate nodes to find connections:**

   - From \( E \), we have paths through nodes A, B, and J. We need to explore their connections:
     - \( A \) connects to F, S, and B, of which B is already a neighbor of E.
     - \( B \) connects to E, A, and C.
     - \( J \) connects to B and H.

   - From \( R \), we have paths through nodes P, Q, O, and G. We need to explore their connections:
     - \( P \) connects to Q, I, and S.
     - \( Q \) connects to P, I, and R (already considered), and G.
     - \( O \) connects to R and C.
     - \( G \) connects to Q, S, and C.
   - Nodes C and B seem to be potential middle nodes. Let’s analyze the path lengths involving these nodes.

5. **Trace potential paths:**
   - Path via C:
     - \( E \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \rightarrow R \)
   - Path via G:
     - \( E \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \rightarrow R \)

   As we see, paths:
   - \( E \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \rightarrow R \)

6. **Determine the shortest path:**
   - The path \( E \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \rightarrow R \) involves 4 edges.

Thus, the shortest path between node \( E \) and node \( R \) is:

Length: 4, Path: [E, B, C, G, R]
